### F2. The six items, with the closest published work and the limits

**Item 1. Lifetime against beta (WP-A).** `PROJECT DATA` (`outputs/session2/wpA/lifetime_table_v2.json`, `S6`; F-010, F-012 and the outcomes note after F-012): 100 long runs, T to death or 30,000, seven to 300,000, radial solver; the longest-lived start at each beta; none of the best starts above 4/3 died by 30,000; retained fraction at T = 300,000: 0.9884 (beta 1.6), 0.9882 (2.0), 0.9842 and 0.9694 (3.0, two starts), 0.9984 and 0.9686 (5.0, two starts). At beta = 5 the windowed rate is consistent with zero for both starts: (1.34 to 1.41)e-10 with error 1.28e-10 for omega0 = 0.9756, and -2.5e-11 to +2.7e-11 with error 6e-11 to 6.5e-11 for omega0 = 0.99119; the retained fractions equal their T = 30,000 values (0.9986, 0.9687) to 2e-4, so the losses of 0.2% and 3% happened before T = 30,000. Nothing above 4/3 died, so permanence (A5) was not tested and the lifetime ordering above the cap is censored. What the sources have: only beta = 4/3 lifetimes for this model (Mukaida 2017, Ibe 2019, Li 2025, all radial, `READ` in D1); a large-g argument for flat-top oscillons (Amin & Shirokoff 2010, `READ`, no numbers) and lattice runs at beta of 17 to 33 in an expanding background (Amin, Easther, Finkel 2010, `READ`, no scan against g); a sextic-coupling scan in a Higgs-like potential, 6e3 at chi_6 = 0 to above 2e5, non-monotone (Drogosz et al. 2026, `READ` in A2 and S3.1); a different "phi^6" model with degenerate vacua, bubbles around phi_vac = -1, one value epsilon = 1, radial, 3+1D, oscillon phase lasting to t of about 1.7e5, retaining about 54% of the initial bubble energy ([Mendonca & de Oliveira 2022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.07900), `READ`, sec. IV; found in search 114, not in the earlier record); indirect lifetimes of at least 1e7 oscillations in 2D for phi^4 and sine-Gordon ([Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0606016), `ABSTRACT`); much longer lifetimes in other potentials (Olle 2020 and 2021, Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron 2021, `READ`, S3.1). None gives lifetime against the sextic-to-quartic ratio in this potential. Limits on the wording: "longest-lived start found" (a lower envelope of what was sampled; the dips are narrower than the start spacing, F-010); radial symmetry only (the 3D check is beta = 4/3 and T = 3000); the cap is 300,000 for seven runs and 30,000 for the rest. The sentence "lifetime rises with beta and does not fall anywhere up to beta = 5" in the report's verdict should read "no fall seen in the starts tried".

**Item 2. The beta = 4/3 state near omega = 0.907 (WP-A, A6 and A9).** `PROJECT DATA` (`long_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json`; F-011; `outputs/session2/wpA/confirm3d/a9_comparison.json` as quoted in `SESSION_02_REPORT.md` section 3): omega0 = 0.906505, settled omega 0.90689 to 0.90707; retained fraction 0.99673, 0.99702, 0.99569, 0.99460 at T = 30k, 100k, 200k, 300k; windowed loss rates 3.3e-9, 4.2e-9, 6.9e-9, 1.67e-8 (error 1.7e-9 each; the draft's "4e-9 to 1.7e-8" starts at the second block, the first is 3.3e-9); doubled box 5.3e-6. 3D (N = 128, L = 70) to T = 3000, tracking the radial curve to 7.0e-5 (frac 0.997268, 0.997275, 0.996686 at T = 1000, 2000, 3000 against 0.997256, 0.997283, 0.996683), which is 435 oscillations. The limit set by [Li, Yamaguchi & Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) (Fig. 3, `READ (figure)`, `PRIOR_ART.md` D1 and D2): their omega_ini = 0.90 run, in the same potential (m^2 g6/lambda^2 = 16/15, matched by N25), is plotted alive at m t of about 5e5 at lambda E/m of about 37 (the project's lump is 36.85; positions agree within the 4% panel mismatch noted in D1), with no lifetime number in the text. So a very long-lived beta = 4/3 lump is not new, and neither is a lifetime beyond 3e5. What is not in the record of sources: the lowest plotted rates are about 4.5e-8 (Li semi-analytic dip), 9e-8 (Ibe) and about 5e-9 (the floor of Li's numerical panel), so a measured rate of 3.3e-9 is below anything I found; no source found for a measured retained fraction at T = 300,000, and no source found for a 3D (non-spherical) test of this potential in flat space (the 3D lattice runs found, Antusch et al. 2019 and Amin, Easther, Finkel 2010, are in an expanding background with other parameters). Also: Mukaida's toy calculation reaches 1e8 to 1e9 (`READ`, D1) and exact long-lived oscillons exist for non-polynomial potentials (Olle 2021), neither a measurement for this potential. Limits: the "near omega = 0.907" is the start; the lump settles at 0.9069 (0.9071 at 300k); positions in omega against analytic curves carry the 1.5e-3 offset of N23; the 3D test is short. Wording: "T = 3000 on a grid".

**Item 3. The measured pair force (WP-C).** `PROJECT DATA` (`outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/summary.json`, `criteria.json`; F-013, F-014 and the WP-C outcomes note): decay constant 0.4172 from sliding windows (0.4169 centroid measure; the reviewer 0.4163) against 0.41647; amplitude 21,137 (reviewer 20,744) against 2 pi A^2 = 20,200; force at 90 degrees follows F0 cos(measured dtheta) within 0.055 F0 (C5a); N flows from the phase leader to the lagger at 1.571 and 0.1765 per unit time at d = 16 and 20 (0.98 and 0.72 of the predicted rates over t = 15 to 60) and at 0.99 to 1.08 of the prediction at d = 24 from t = 60 (the early window at d = 24 is the F-014 miss); the phase runs toward 180 degrees, ending at 215.4, 159.6, 106.5 degrees at the end of the 90-degree pairs. Departures that belong in the same sentence: windows near contact and the 1.3 to 1.5 excess in the 45-degree pair at d = 16 (F-013). What the sources have: the lowest-order potential for identical 2D and 3D solitons ([Malomed 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9809009), `READ`); the phase pendulum and the charge flow from the phase-leading to the lagging Q-ball ([Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252), `READ`; mechanical model, no force measured); Q-ball forces in 1+1D ([Bowcock et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0809.3895), `READ`); 1+1D oscillons: "the force between oscillons decays exponentially at large separation, with a strength modulated by their relative phase" ([Campos, Manton, Mohammadi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633), `ABSTRACT` for this sentence, the fetch-tool summary of the abstract, the summary of the abstract also says the oscillons are built from the Fodor expansion and the simulations are collisions, from which I take the force as derived and not measured, which I did not check in the text; I read other parts of v1 in C1); 3D oscillon collisions with phase-dependent outcomes and no force ([Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337), [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597), `READ`); a variational energy of two superposed non-relativistic solitons against separation and relative phase, with gravity and self-interaction, Gaussian trial states ([Cotner 2018](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/8kt7j20g), UCLA dissertation, sec. 2.3, `READ`, found in search 110; an effective potential, not a measured force); the population transfer between 3D bright solitons varying as sin(delta phi) ([Parker et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0712.3002), `READ`); the tail method for Q-ball forces ([Zhou 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16604), `READ`, E1). No source found for a force measured between real 3D oscillons against separation and phase, or for the N exchange rate, the direction rule or the drift toward 180 degrees between real oscillons (searches 59 to 63, 89 to 96, 102, 103, 107, 108, 110, 117, 118; each returned collision outcomes, complex-field solitons, 1+1D oscillons or gravity-bound solitons). Limits: lumps are copies of one settled lump, one beta, one box and grid (WP-C resolution and box checks 1.6e-6 and 4.8e-4 in d(t)); the law is the project's derivation of a published method.

**Item 4. The phase dependence of the start (F-009).** `DERIVED` N22 and N23 (above, Addendum C) with the project files: slice energy of the single-frequency ansatz for the primary (beta = 4/3, omega = 0.909149, E_bar = 2801.68): 2843.83 at theta = 0, 2861.28 at 22.5, 2878.85 at 45, 2742.08 at 67.5, 2605.21 at 90 (peak to peak 9.8%); settled omega predicted from it, 0.90811, 0.90769, 0.90728, 0.91062, 0.91428 against measured 0.90952, 0.90923, 0.90875, 0.91215, 0.91611, a constant offset of 1.4e-3 to 1.8e-3 with a spread of 0.4e-3 against a spread over phases of 7.4e-3. Related and `READ` (Addendum C, C1): [Gálvez Ghersi & Braden 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04750) (an exact breather started at phase theta_0 in a deformed equation: initial energy independent of theta_0 in D = 1; cusps at theta_0 = pi/2 in the settled frequency), Campos et al. (phase-dependent deviation from the field equation for truncated-expansion pairs), Battye & Sutcliffe (the charge of an ansatz pair depends on the phase), [van Dissel et al. 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072) (one starting phase, `READ` in C1; a re-fetch of the page in this round, by the fetch tool, found no study of the starting phase). No source found for the phase dependence of the slice energy of a single-frequency real-field start, or for predicting the settled frequency from it (searches 84, 85, 97, 98, 109). This is a consequence of the structure (the energy of the slice is a function of the fundamental alone and oscillates at 2, 4 and 6 times the phase), derived here after the fact: the record should say that the project missed it before the run (F-009), and use no word stronger than "derived". The check in N23 has five points at one beta and a constant unexplained offset.

**Item 5. Arrangements (WP-D, WP-E).**
- (a) *The exclusion in the first-order pair law.* The pair structure is published: attraction for relative phase below 90 degrees (cos > 0); a phase equation with a stable point at 180 and an unstable point at 0 (Battye & Sutcliffe 2000, `READ`; the negative effective mass of the phase in Malomed 1998, `READ`; the non-rigid pendulum, Raghavan et al. 1999, `READ`); and the statement that stepped-phase clusters are metastable and break symmetry (Desyatnikov & Kivshar 2002, `READ`). The statement "for uniform stepped rings attraction between neighbours and stability of their relative phase exclude each other" is the WP-D review's own result (`WPD_NOTES.md`, review corrections, with steps below 90 attracting and unstable, steps of 90 and above stable and not attracting). Closest: [Dong, Liu & Malomed 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22231) (`READ`): steps up to 90 degrees attract and steps between 90 and 270 degrees repel; clusters of the attractive kind need angular momentum and a radius near the energy minimum to persist. No source found for the exclusion stated for rings or other arrangements, after searches 16, 43, 54, 71, 77, 78, 89 to 96, 111, 116, 117. State it as "derived here for the first-order tail law, with the pair case published".
- (b) *The point-lump scan.* `PROJECT DATA` (`WPD_NOTES.md` lines 7 to 9, 80 to 95, 454; `outputs/session2/wpD/`): 595 patterns (312 0/180 classes and 283 distinct stepped patterns, counts checked by Burnside and brute force), 28 force balances (5 in Model A, 23 in Model B), none stable once positions, phases and N move; 27 of 28 inside 3.6 hw and 25 of 28 inside 2.5 hw. The scan follows the rigid one-parameter (radial) family of each pattern; 58 of the 595 are radial by symmetry and 537 are not, so a non-radial equilibrium is not excluded; Model B's phase and N sector is unvalidated. Closest published: effective-potential minima for ring clusters (DK 2002; Li, Malomed, Mihalache 2020; Dong et al. 2025, all `READ`), a variational two-soliton energy (Cotner 2018, `READ`), and the boson-star and Q-ball chains of S1.4 (complex fields, `READ` or `ABSTRACT` as tagged there). No source found for a systematic scan of phase patterns over rings and polyhedra in a point-lump model, after searches 17, 64, 66, 77, 78, 111, 116. Wording: "no stable balance in the radial family of 595 patterns, in a model valid for lumps beyond about 3.6 hw".
- (c) *The 3D tests.* `PROJECT DATA` (F-017 to F-019 and the WP-E outcomes note): 14 runs E1 to E14 at beta = 4/3, dx = 0.55, rings of 3 to 8 lumps and shells, stepped and 0/180 phases; none ended as a stable set of K lumps. The bonded rule labels E11 and E14 bonded; E14 is the control and E11 was contracting with no turning point when it stopped (F-017); its second run (T = 1200, finer grid) is pending, so "nothing bonds" is not yet a result. Published: stepped-phase clusters in 3D exist for complex fields, [Crasovan et al. 2003](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0301029) (`READ`, 3D two-colour clusters with competing quadratic and cubic terms, durations up to thousands of diffraction lengths, S5) and Mihalache et al. 2004 (`ABSTRACT`, 3D cubic-quintic clusters) and Dong, Liu & Malomed 2025 (`READ`, 3D quantum-droplet necklaces that persist to t above 1500, about 10 diffraction times, under random perturbations of up to 5% when started at the energy-minimum radius); the cubic-quintic literature that says no persistent bound states of 3D solitons form in free space (Burlak & Malomed 2018, `READ`); a published exact stationary cluster at a 90-degree step (N = 8, m = 2, DK 2002, 2D saturable, complex). In all of these the balance is attraction against angular momentum in a complex field. For real-field oscillon arrangements, no source found (searches 17, 64, 66, 69, 77, 78, 111, 118); the Wikipedia statements about oscillon "molecules" and chains (opposite phases attract, like phases repel) remain untraced and are the reverse of the measured pair law (search 78, S4). Limits: one beta, one lump size, one grid; the resolution and box checks cover E3 only and only up to contact (F-018); the rule was flawed (F-017).

**Item 6. Multi-lump mergers (WP-E).** `PROJECT DATA` (reviewer's table, `WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md` last section; F-018): E1 (5-ring, 72-degree steps, a = 16): pair within 2 hw at 294, count 5 to 3 to 2, two lumps of 0.36 E0 each (omega about 0.87) leave back to back at v = 0.13, 0.72 E0 in lumps; E2 (5-ring in phase): contact 150 to 160, one compressed lump at 160, two lumps of 0.38 E0 each (v = 0.10, omega 0.867) leave along plus and minus z, perpendicular to the ring, 0.76 E0 in lumps (the report quotes 0.75 at T = 400); deeper overlaps give two lumps of 0.41 E0 (E3) and 0.45 E0 (E7). E8 (octahedron around an anti-phase centre, R = 3.82 = 1.14 hw): one lump from t of about 25 to 35 with 0.47 E0 at T = 300, omega 0.83, losing 0.23% of itself per unit time, its N falling from 24,100 to 18,300. The sources: two-body mergers of real oscillons lose a fraction of the energy and give a lower-frequency oscillon (Amin & Mou 2020, `READ` for the set-up; a loss of about 30% in the snippet of search 113 is `SECONDHAND`); for rings, unstable rotating clusters break into several fundamental solitons or fuse into a nearly axisymmetric vortex torus (DK 2002 and Li, Malomed, Mihalache 2020, `READ`); in the 3D quantum-droplet model a ring without angular momentum fuses gradually "into a fundamental state elongated along the z-direction" (Dong et al. 2025, `READ`, sec. 3, Fig. 3a, complex field; no ejected pair is described in the text); 2D flat-top solitons in the cubic-quintic equation merge when in phase ([Phase-controlled collisions, 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07762), `ABSTRACT`, two-body only). No source found for the outcome of a simultaneous merger of five or more real-field lumps, for two lumps ejected back to back along the axis of an in-phase ring, or for the energy left in one lump by a shell around an anti-phase centre (searches 18, 62, 112, 113, 115). The Dong et al. elongation along the ring axis is a partial anticipation of the axial ejection and should be cited next to it. Limits: each outcome is a single run at dx = 0.55 with the sponge at 18 or more from the lumps; the remnant energies (0.72 to 0.76, 0.47) are read before the lumps leave the box or while the remnant is still losing energy; no resolution or box check exists after contact (F-018 (d)).

### F5. Not read, open

- Mendonca & de Oliveira 2022: the figures of the energy against time were not examined; t_max values are the text's. Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006, Campos et al. 2026 (the force section beyond the abstract), 2602.07762 and the other abstract-level sources in F3 were not opened beyond their abstract pages.
- Crasovan et al. 2003 and Mihalache et al. 2004: the durations of the 3D cubic-quintic clusters in the stated regime are not known to me beyond S5 and S10. The figure in Dong et al. 2025 (Fig. 3, Fig. 4) was not examined, only the text.
- The statement of the Wikipedia oscillon page that oscillons form "molecules" and "long chains", with opposite phases attracting, has no source I could trace (searches 11, 78, 111, 118) and conflicts with the measured and derived pair law.
- Whether a published stepped-ring cluster in a real field exists in a context I did not search (Skyrmion or vortex rings, or the Q-ball literature on spinning chains): the searches above found complex-field cases only.

---

## Session 3 brief

Scope: WP-S of `SESSION_03_PLAN.md`: what published work says about WP-L (break test of a lone lump), WP-P (unequal lumps and orbital motion), and the literature check of the held second field. Written 2026-10-02 for the orchestrator before the WP-L and WP-P runs. Model claude-sonnet-5-5. Effort: a "reasoning_effort" value of 30 is visible in my context; the mapping from that number to a named level is not visible (`CLAUDE.md` lists xhigh for this worker). No git commands, no simulations, nothing written outside `docs/` (the extracted texts and a small Swift PDF-to-text script stayed in the session scratchpad).

Tags as in the earlier briefs: `READ`, `ABSTRACT`, `SECONDHAND`, `DERIVED`, `PROJECT DATA`, "no source found". Numbers carry their parameters; a number from another potential, dimension or speed regime is context and says so. Nothing is quoted; every statement is a paraphrase.

How sources were read. As in Session 2: the fetch tool saved the PDF of each paper it could not convert, and I extracted the text locally (PDFKit) and searched it. `READ` means I read the passage in that extracted text. The fetch tool's own summaries were used only to find the saved file. Search-engine summaries are `SECONDHAND` and are used here only as pointers. Sections are T0 to T11; `DERIVED` notes continue as N31 to N35; searches continue as 120 to 151. Unit systems differ between the papers; each row gives the paper's own units.

### T0. Headline findings

1. **No published break threshold for a real-field 3D oscillon, in any variable.** No source found for an amplitude kick, a removal of N, or an incoming wave packet applied to a settled lump of the real phi^4-phi^6 family (searches 120 to 122, 142, 146, 147). The nearest numbers come from neighbouring systems (T1): scaling the amplitude of a stable-branch lump by 0.99 or 1.001 relaxes back in the 3D radial cubic-quintic NLS ([Carles, Klein, Sparber 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.11637) `READ`); complex 2+1D Q-balls relax from random kicks that add up to 40% of their energy, at about 0.02 to 0.06 per unit time, and keep their charge ([Multamaki 2001](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0102339) `READ`); an elliptical kick of a 2D real oscillon decays and the core loses a little over 10% ([Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0606016) `READ`).
2. **The only threshold published as a fractional change of charge** is a sign rule on the unstable branch of the 3D cubic-quintic NLS: scaling the amplitude by 0.999 (mass about 0.2% lower) disperses the lump, scaling it by 1.001 (mass about 0.2% higher) relaxes it to a stable lump with about 3% less mass (Carles et al. `READ`). On the stable branch no threshold appears for amplitude scalings of 0.99 and 1.001. For the sextic oscillon the floor is the critical charge N_min (Mukaida 2017 and Ibe 2019 `READ` in Session 1; a lump driven to N_min dies fast). `DERIVED` (N31): the imposed removal that can break a lump outright is below 1 - N_min/N, which is 0.45 (dip start), 0.42 (Session 1 primary) and 0.49 (beta = 5 start); the real threshold is lower because the relaxation sheds more N.
3. **Kicks near a dip: the sign matters** (N32). A lump radiates, so N only falls and omega only rises along its life; the published lifetime picture has a lump that starts on the high-N side of a dip spend most of its life in the dip ([Zhang 2020b](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.11720) `READ`, Session 2 D1; Ibe eq. 55; Mukaida's attractor points `READ`). A kick that lowers N past the dip takes the lump out of it for good; a kick that raises N puts it back on the high-N side. The size of the effect is visible in Li 2025 Fig. 3 (`READ (figure)`, Addendum D1): initial energies of about 35 and 41 (a 17% difference) end at a death near 2.1e4 and a lump still alive at 5e5. This half-agrees with the plan's "the dip start is the most fragile".
4. **Collisions.** Identical real 3D oscillons at slow speed: in phase merges, anti-phase bounces ([Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597), each lump 0.01 to 0.02 c, `READ`; [Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337), in-phase merger up to a head-on relative speed of 0.1 c, `READ`). The merger keeps about 70% of the energy and leaves an excited lump. At high speed lumps pass through (2D real oscillons at v = 0.5, Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006 `READ`; Q-balls at v = 0.8, [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) `READ`). In-between phases give charge transfer for Q-balls (`READ`) and a recoiling merger in the real 3D case (Amin & Mou, stated without a run). Nothing for the sextic family. Mismatched lumps: outcome effectively stochastic (Xue).
5. **Unequal frequencies.** The first-order cosine force has zero mean: the pair interaction energy oscillates at the frequency difference, with a small constant remainder ([Axenides et al. 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9910388) `READ`); for Q-balls of unequal frequency the initial relative phase is not preserved, and at rest they repel with almost no charge transfer; a collision that brings them through each other transfers charge by an amount that depends on the phase at contact (Battye & Sutcliffe 2000, 1D, `READ`). A second-order, phase-independent attraction is published for NLS-type systems and does bind or spiral them: a bound breathing pair in 1D ([Al Khawaja 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1004.3000) `READ`) and stable spiraling in 3D saturable NLS ([Buryak, Kivshar, Shih, Segev 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9812005) `READ`). The plan's guess "the first-order force averages to about zero" is confirmed; "neither bind nor merge quickly" is only half supported (T4, T7). For real Klein-Gordon lumps: no source found.
6. **Orbits.** No orbiting pair or ring of real-field lumps, and no complex Klein-Gordon pair stable over many orbits, was found. Every published stable orbit has an extra ingredient: a second incoherent component (Buryak et al.), nonlocality ([Lopez-Aguayo et al. 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0512053) `READ`), a trap ([Burlak & Malomed 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.09173), Session 2 `READ`), or angular momentum with a conserved complex envelope, which gives metastable rings (Desyatnikov & Kivshar 2002, Session 2 `READ`). For phase-coherent solitons the effective mass of the relative phase is negative and spiraling bound states are saddles ([Kivshar, Desyatnikov, Torner review 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0501026) sec. 6.1 `READ`; Malomed 1998, Session 2 `READ`).
7. **Held second field (T6).** Q-balls are absolutely stable for charges above Q_S (energy below mQ), classically stable but quantum-metastable between Q_C and Q_S, and classically unstable on the upper branch; for thick-wall lumps it depends on the potential and the dimension ([Zhou 2024 review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16604) `READ`; [Tsumagari, Copeland, Saffin 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0805.3233) `READ`). A classical lump with an exactly conserved charge has no classical decay channel, so it conflicts with "metastable" unless the charge conservation is broken; a real oscillon decays because N is only an adiabatic invariant.
8. **Models with bound unequal lumps and a metastable lone lump.** The closest is the real Friedberg-Lee-Sirlin model: co-located oscillons of two real fields with different frequencies, each with its own approximately conserved adiabatic invariant, read as a bound state of two oscillons ([Murai, Ogawa, Takahashi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04494) `READ`). For stepped-phase clusters of lumps whose lone member is metastable: no source found; all published stepped-phase clusters are complex fields whose lone lumps are stable.
9. **Pitfall for L2 (boosted lumps).** A Galilean boost of oscillon data gives a speed that depends on the oscillon's phase and shape; a target speed of 0.02 c needed a nominal boost velocity near 0.4 c in one case, and the fix suggested is a Lorentz boost that mixes time slices (Xue et al. 2025, Sec. III, `READ`). The plan's L2 check (speed, energy gamma E0, frequency omega/gamma) is the right gate.
10. **Correction to the Session 1 and 2 briefs.** The paper [arXiv:2001.07476](https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.07476) that I cited for "small relative phase differences merge" is not about collisions (its title is "Axion star nucleation in dark minihalos around primordial black holes", `READ`, introduction). Amin & Mou 2020 cite their source for that statement by a reference number I could not resolve from the extracted text; the statement is theirs (T3).

Not found: a threshold of break, in any variable, for a settled real-field lump (T0 item 1); a published removal of N from a real oscillon. For complex fields the nearest physical processes are named in the introduction of Multamaki 2001 (`READ`): dissociation by hard collisions, dissolution of the soft edge, evaporation by pair production at the surface (Cohen et al. 1986, not obtained). The mathematical statement that a small perturbation of a stable soliton relaxes to a nearby soliton plus dispersing radiation is the asymptotic-stability theory of Soffer & Weinstein and Buslaev & Perelman (`SECONDHAND`: search 148, survey [arXiv:2009.00573](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.00573) not opened).

### T6. The held second field: Q-ball stability and multi-component models

| Source | Statement | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| [Zhou 2024 review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16604), "Stability" and "Tunnelling of classically stable Q-balls" | The energy-charge curve of 3+1D Q-balls with negative quartic coupling has a cusp C and a charge Q_S where E = mQ. Near omega -> m the energy exceeds mQ and the lump is unstable (upper branch, a barrier peak); a lower branch needs dQ/domega <= 0. For Q above Q_S the energy is below mQ and the lump is stable even against emitting quanta; Coleman proved it is the absolute minimum under a condition on the potential. Between Q_C and Q_S the lump is classically stable but quantum mechanically unstable; it can decay by tunnelling with rate A exp(-F), F about Q_C for large Q_C, which can exceed the age of the universe. In 2+1D the curve has no part above E = mQ and meets it at a finite Q_S; in 1+1D at Q = 0 | READ |
| [Tsumagari, Copeland, Saffin 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0805.3233), secs. 1, 2.4, 5 | Absolute stability needs E below the energy of the same number of free quanta; classical stability needs dQ/domega < 0 (the same condition as stability against fission). Three types: absolutely stable, metastable (decays into free quanta, stable under small fluctuations) and unstable (Q-clouds). Thin-wall Q-balls are always classically stable; thick-wall classical stability depends on the model and the dimension (for n = 4 polynomial potentials thick-wall lumps in D >= 3 are classically unstable); higher dimensions are more stable | READ |
| [Multamaki & Vilja 1999](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9908446), secs. 2 and 4 | the condition E < mQ for a stable Q-ball; stability of thick-wall Q-balls depends strongly on the form of the potential; when the field couples to other fields (for example massless fermions) E < mQ no longer guarantees stability (evaporation) | READ |
| [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252), sec. 2 | for their potential E/Q falls with Q, so the lumps are stable against fission into smaller ones; in D = 3 there is a lower bound on the charge, in D = 1 none; this is potential-dependent | READ |
| Mukaida et al. 2017; Ibe et al. 2019 (Session 1 section 2.7) | an oscillon is the real-field projection of a Q-ball: an approximate U(1) and an adiabatic invariant that stands in for the charge; the decay is the number-violating radiation | READ |
| [Murai, Ogawa, Takahashi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04494), introduction | contrasts Q-balls (stability from an exact U(1)) with oscillons (approximate conservation of an adiabatic invariant); builds the real version of the Friedberg-Lee-Sirlin model (the complex field of the original model replaced by a real one) and finds single-field oscillons and two-field co-located oscillons with different frequencies | READ |
| Copeland et al. 2014; Alonso-Izquierdo et al. 2025 (Session 2 S1.4) | charge-swapping Q-balls are not absolutely stable but live at least about 1e4 periods; a later reading treats them as a real oscillon with an excitation in the imaginary direction | READ; ABSTRACT |
| [Jaramillo & Zhou 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08985) (Session 2 S1.4) | the flat-space Friedberg-Lee-Sirlin model (complex plus real mediator) has static dipoles and chains of Q-balls; the lone Q-ball there is a stable Q-ball | READ |
| [van Dissel & Sfakianakis 2022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.07789) | symmetric two-field and multifield oscillons with identical sextic potentials: decay rates and lifetimes like the single-field ones | SECONDHAND (search 136; not opened) |
| Desyatnikov & Kivshar 2002; Crasovan et al. 2003; Dong, Liu, Malomed 2025 (Session 2) | stepped-phase clusters exist in complex fields whose lone lumps are stable (conserved power or particle number); the vector (two-component) clusters need an incoherent second component | READ |

`DERIVED` N35 (what this means for "a lone swirl is metastable"). (i) With an exactly conserved charge and E below mQ, a classical lone lump on the stable branch has no decay channel: it is permanent in a classical simulation, and "metastable" would mean only the quantum tunnelling of Zhou's review, or an evaporation channel into another sector (Multamaki & Vilja). (ii) A real oscillon is metastable because its N is only approximately conserved. (iii) The project's real lumps have E below mN: `PROJECT DATA` N25 gives E = 2913 and N = 149.4 x 20 = 2988 for the dip start (E/(mN) = 0.975), and Li 2025 Fig. 2 (`READ (figure)`, Session 1 section 2.4) gives E/(mN) about 1.0 at the N-minimum and about 0.9 at omega = 0.85, so most of the stable branch lies below mN. If a complex partner had an energy-charge curve like this one, its lumps would be absolutely stable, which conflicts with "metastable". This is an inference from the structure, not a computation: the complex partner's profile equation has different coefficients from the real one (the real field averages cos^4 and cos^6), so its curve is not the same. (iv) Two ways to keep a lone lump metastable while adding a second component: a real second field (Murai et al.), or a complex component with a small U(1)-breaking term (the logic of Mukaida et al.); no source found that tests either for bound clusters.

A 17% change in initial energy changed a published lifetime by more than 25 times (Li Fig. 3). beta = 5: no published lifetime or perturbation data; N/N_min is 1.97 against 1.83, which gives a slightly larger margin to the floor (N31) | Half agrees; the sign asymmetry is a testable prediction; beta = 5 "most robust" rests on nothing found, apart from the Session 2 measurements |
| WP-L (a), kicks of +-epsilon | not stated beyond the definitions | 3D NLS and Q-ball sources above: amplitude scalings of 0.99 and 1.001 (NLS) and random kicks up to 10% of the peak (Q-balls) relax; no real-field threshold found. A kick of epsilon in the field and velocity changes N by about 2 epsilon at leading order (N31) | No source for thresholds; expect relaxation with shedding below the floor |
| WP-L (b), N removed from the core | "broken outright" if the energy inside 3 hw falls below half within 2000 time units | Lumps break by reaching N_min (N31). Losing half the energy within 2000 time units needs a rate of about 3.5e-4 (ln 2 / 2000, for exponential decay); Ibe's rate peak just above the floor is about 1e-4 at Q about 100, log10 Q = 2.0 (`READ (figure)`, Addendum D1), which is lower; so only a removal that reaches the floor is expected to break a lump within 2000, and removals of a few percent only change its rate | Consistent; use the project's own Gamma(N) map rather than the published one |

Plane waves push a lump and leave it intact (Xue; Ciurla); resonant growth exists in 1D (Romanczukiewicz & Shnir). N33: k3 = 2.529, 2.537, 2.751; the lump's 3 omega source vanishes at a dip, so coupling there is weaker | Rests on nothing found for the 3D sextic case |
| WP-L (d), quadrupolar and no-symmetry distortions | the dip start gets its first generic-perturbation test | elliptical and generic kicks relax (Hindmarsh & Salmi; Kinach & Choptuik; Session 1 run 5); higher l more stable, low l case dependent (Session 1, appendix C of Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron); a narrow Floquet band near k about sqrt(3) m is unresolved (Session 1, Amin & Shirokoff) | Consistent; the expected effect is degradation through shed N, not a break |

Conflicts with the unqualified statement at high speed (pass-through) and for unequal lumps (stochastic); 90 degrees: charge transfer and, in the NLS limit, repulsion with an N asymmetry up to 0.76 (T3); the project's own pair runs showed repulsion at 90 degrees from rest | Agrees at slow speed; add the speed and equality qualifiers |

Second half: published NLS-type unequal pairs do bind (1D, Al Khawaja) or spiral (3D incoherent, Buryak) through a second-order attraction, but with the range e^(-2 kappa R) instead of e^(-kappa R); unequal Q-balls at rest repelled in 1D (Battye & Sutcliffe). No published orbit of real-field lumps; every stable orbit has an added ingredient (T5) | First half agrees; second half half-supported; "orbits are the likelier route" has no source for a real field |

### T8. Notes for WP-L and WP-P

`DERIVED` notes, with the arithmetic shown. Re-derive before relying on them.

- **N31 (the floor and the size of a kick).** Take N/N_min from Session 2: 1.83 for the dip start (omega = 0.9065), 1.73 for the Session 1 primary (0.9091), 1.97 for the beta = 5 start (0.9756). A lump whose N is driven to N_min dies fast (Mukaida, Ibe). The imposed fractional removal 1 - N_min/N is therefore an upper bound on the removal needed to break it: 0.4536, 0.4220, 0.4924. The relaxation sheds more N, so the true threshold is lower. Scaling phi and its velocity by 1 + epsilon scales the oscillation amplitude by 1 + epsilon and N by (1 + epsilon)^2 at leading order, so Delta N / N is about 2 epsilon and the matching epsilon is 0.23, 0.21, 0.25 (the lump's own omega also shifts, so this is a guide only).
- **N32 (the dip and the sign of a kick).** On the adiabatic path N falls and omega rises (Ibe eq. 55; Li eq. 2.30; Zhang 2020b sec. 2.1). A lump on the high-N side of a dip passes into it; one placed on the low-N side cannot return. A kick of one sign puts the lump on one side. The Session 2 dip is about 1.7% wide in N (F-011), so a removal of a few percent moves the dip start out of its dip and an addition of a few percent does not, as long as the excess N is not shed quickly. A kick also excites shape modes that radiate at about the rates found for complex lumps (Multamaki: relaxation over tens of time units).
- **N33 (wavenumbers, couplings and geometry).** With m = 1, k_n = sqrt((n omega)^2 - 1): n = 2: 1.512, 1.519, 1.675; n = 3 (the plan's k3): 2.529, 2.537, 2.751; n = 5: 4.421, 4.434, 4.774, for omega = 0.9065, 0.9091, 0.9756. The potential is even, so the perturbation equation of the lump has a modulation of period T/2, and the Floquet resonances of a perturbation of frequency w_k sit at w_k = n omega, so n = 2 corresponds to the band that A&S place near k about sqrt(3) m (Session 1: narrowest bands there; omega near 1 gives k_2 = sqrt(3); Zhang also found short-wavelength Floquet instabilities analytically, fate unclear); k_2 is a third wavenumber the plan could add, with no source on its fate. At a rate dip the 3 omega source of the lump nearly vanishes (Zhang 2020; Ibe), so a packet at k_3 couples weakly to the dip start at first order and strongly to the outside-the-dip primary; at the dip the 5 omega channel sets the rate, so k_5 is the matching test. An incoming wave at the lump's own 3 omega adds to the emitted 3 omega field coherently, so the energy exchange is first order in its amplitude and depends on its phase (feeds or drains), while at a slow wavenumber the exchange is second order (Ciurla; Xue push). A spherical packet carries no net momentum, so it cannot push the lump; its amplitude grows as 1/r on the way in, so the lump sees a larger field than was launched.
- **N34 (what a point model of unequal lumps lacks).** For two lumps phi_1 + phi_2 of different omega the cross term of the quadratic part of the energy oscillates at omega_1 - omega_2 with zero mean (this is the cos term of the Session 2 law). The cross term of the quartic part has a nonzero mean, -(g4/16) times the integral of Phi_1^2 Phi_2^2 over space (profiles Phi_i, phi_i = Phi_i cos(omega_i t + theta_i); attractive for g4 > 0), which for exponential tails falls roughly as exp(-2 kappa_min R) times a power of R, set by the smaller kappa; the sextic cross terms are repulsive and higher order. The oscillating force also produces a second-order secular term of the same order (the sign was not worked out here). The published analogues are the incoherent attraction of Buryak et al. and the constant part of the force of Al Khawaja. Neither is in the Session 2 pair law, so WP-P1's unequal-lump scan is incomplete without it. Single-term algebra, not a checked derivation.
- **N35** is in T6.

Practical implications, each tied to its source.

1. **L2.** Use a boost that mixes time slices or check the boosted lump's speed from momentum over energy, v = P/E, as Xue et al. do (their eq. 9); the speed depends on phase and shape; expect a nominal boost far above the speed obtained.
2. **L1 (a), (b).** Report both the imposed Delta N / N and the N shed in the first few hundred time units; scan removals from a few percent to 50% of N and kicks of both signs; compare the dip start with the primary at equal imposed Delta N / N, and score by the sign (N32).
3. **L1 (c).** Use the small amplitude of the published plane-wave runs (a few percent of the peak, Xue) for the first packet; launch packets at the slow wavenumber, k_2, k_3 and k_5 (N33); a spherical shell focuses.
4. **L3 (e).** Published slow-speed regime is up to 0.1 c head-on (Amin & Mou) and 0.01 to 0.02 c each (Xue); pass-through appears near 0.5 c in 2D and 0.8 in the Q-ball units; two speeds such as 0.05 c and 0.3 c would straddle the published merger regime, with the real threshold unknown for this potential. Copies and mirror copies (field and derivative times -1) for phase 0 and pi, as in Xue and Session 2; for 90 degrees use the settled-lump time-shift tool; expect charge transfer or repulsion, not a merger, at slow speed.
5. **P1.** Add the second-order term of N34 or state that the model lacks it; the Session 2 point model has only the coherent law.
6. **P2.** Published stable spirals depend on a commensurability between orbit and phase or power-exchange frequencies; record the orbit period and the beat period 2 pi / (omega_1 - omega_2).
7. **P3.** A bonded outcome in a real field would have no published precedent (T9); stricter checks apply.

## Addendum G (2026-10-02, Session 3, after the WP-P1 review): the Yukawa circular-orbit criterion, and the literature check for F-020 to F-022 (searches 152 to 166)

Scope: one source request from the WP-P1 review (`WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 10, first row: "for the scholar to source") and the literature checks for the P2 and P4 misses recorded in `FAILURES.md` F-020 to F-022. Model claude-sonnet-5-5; a "reasoning_effort" value of 30 is visible in my context (CLAUDE.md lists xhigh for this worker; the mapping is not visible). Tags as above. The tool is web search and page fetch: "no source found" means these queries and the pages opened returned nothing. Sources tagged `READ` were converted to text locally (PDFKit) and the passage was read there; the Session 2 and 3 extractions of Axenides, Battye & Sutcliffe, Al Khawaja, Buryak, Xue and Campos were searched again for the passages used in F-020 and F-021. No git commands, no simulations, nothing written outside `docs/` (extracted texts stayed in the session scratchpad). Paraphrase only.

### G1. Circular orbits in a Yukawa potential: unstable beyond kappa r = (1 + sqrt 5)/2

| Statement | Source | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| For a central attractive force F(r), a circular orbit at r0 is stable against radial perturbation if F'(r0)/F(r0) + 3/r0 is positive (their sign convention, F negative for attraction): the familiar non-relativistic condition, their eq. (25), attributed there to Marion's textbook | [Aguirregabiria, Hernandez, Rivas, arXiv:physics/0407049](https://arxiv.org/abs/physics/0407049) (arXiv preprint of 12 July 2004, physics.ed-ph; the listing shows no journal reference), sec. III | READ |
| For the Yukawa potential V = -(k/r) exp(-r/a) the non-relativistic condition reduces to a^2 + a r - r^2 > 0 (their eq. 30); the relativistic condition (their eq. 29) is more restrictive | same paper, sec. IV | READ |
| Stability of circular orbits in the Yukawa potential holds for rho at most 1.62 a (the commutative limit of their noncommutative condition, their eq. 34 and the sentence after it); the authors call this result well known from classical mechanics | [Nozari & Akhshabi, arXiv:gr-qc/0608076](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/0608076), sec. 3.3 (listed as Chaos, Solitons & Fractals 37, 324, 2008, per the arXiv page). The footnote there ties the number to the golden mean through a numerology framework that I do not use | READ |
| The textbook treatment: Marion, Classical Dynamics of Particles and Systems, 2nd ed. (Academic Press, 1970), sec. 8.11, cited by Aguirregabiria et al. for the condition | not opened | SECONDHAND |
| The closed form: solving a^2 + a r - r^2 > 0 with x = r/a gives x^2 - x - 1 < 0, so stable for x below (1 + sqrt 5)/2 = 1.618 and unstable above. With kappa = 1/a this is the statement in the review request, kappa r above (1 + sqrt 5)/2 unstable | my one-line solution of the READ inequality | DERIVED |

Notes for use (DERIVED unless marked).
- The same condition follows from d ln |F| / d ln r > -3 with |F| = k e^{-kappa r} (1 + kappa r) / r^2, which is a check on the algebra, not an extra source.
- The relative coordinate of two bodies in a central pair potential obeys the one-body problem with the reduced mass, so the criterion applies to the separation d. This is standard and I did not source it.
- For the project's lumps kappa = sqrt(1 - omega^2) = 0.4165 at omega = 0.909149, so the threshold is d = 1.618 / 0.4165 = 3.885, which is 1.16 hw (hw = 24.077 / 7.2 = 3.344). This matches the "d > 3.9 = 1.16 hw" in `WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 0.
- What the sources do not cover: both treat a point particle in a static Yukawa potential. The project's equal-lump law has the Yukawa form U = -2 pi A^2 cos D exp(-kappa d)/d only for frozen phases; the unequal-lump law is a sum of two exponentials, and the P1 build's linearization finds phase and N modes in addition. The Yukawa criterion is the radial-mode statement only, and the build's "radial e-fold / T_orb" column is the corresponding model result. The relativistic correction (more restrictive in Aguirregabiria et al.) is of order v^2 at the project's circular speeds of 0.003 to 0.16.
- No source found for a statement of this criterion applied to a pair of solitons or oscillons.

## Addendum H (2026-10-02, Session 3, after the scoring of Block L1): the literature check for F-023 to F-026 (searches 167 to 178)

Scope: the literature checks for the four misses of Block L1 recorded in `FAILURES.md` F-023 (break threshold, L1-1), F-024 (family rule for the rate, L1-5), F-025 (degradation ordering, L1-8) and F-026 (k3 packets, L1-9), and the three questions put with them: is a break at the critical charge after relaxation published, is a lasting excitation at unchanged charge published, is nonlinear absorption of a strong wave by an oscillon or Q-ball published. Model claude-sonnet-5-5; a "reasoning_effort" value of 30 is visible in my context (CLAUDE.md lists xhigh for this worker; the mapping is not visible). Tags as above. The tool is web search and page fetch: "no source found" means these queries and the pages opened returned nothing. As in G, `READ` means the passage was read in text extracted locally from the PDF that the fetch tool saved (the project's `pdf2txt` script, PDFKit); the fetch tool's own summaries were used only to find files, and abstract pages are tagged `ABSTRACT`. No git commands, no simulations, nothing written outside `docs/`. Paraphrase only. The scholar's tabulations from `scan_summary.json` that the entries use are not part of this addendum; they are marked in the entries.

### H1. Sources

| Statement | Source | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| Quasi-breathers are strictly periodic solutions that include a component of inwardly directed radiation, so they are not expected to be physical but approximate an oscillon. Their energy has a minimum at omega_crit that depends on D (D = 3, symmetric double-well phi^4: omega_crit about 0.967). Oscillons with omega below omega_crit move toward it as they radiate and decay once they reach it, because there is no quasi-breather of lower energy (sec. 3.3). A non-fine-tuned Gaussian start (A = 2, sigma = 3.3) sheds energy, has a large modulation of its frequency, approaches the quasi-breather and decays like it, with the decay delayed by about 600 after crossing omega_crit; a quasi-breather start at omega = 0.95 decays near t = 6150 against about 17,000 from the semi-analytic estimate (a factor 3 over). Quasi-breather starts above omega_crit showed no oscillon phase and, except at D = 3, dissipated rapidly | [Saffin & Tranberg 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0610191), secs. 1, 3.3, 4.1, 4.4 | READ |
| An oscillon demises when it reaches its minimum energy state (attributed to Watkins, unpublished, and Saffin & Tranberg), demonstrated in 3D phi^4; the energy and frequency of oscillons from Gaussian data are fitted by a constant plus a decaying radiative power law in time; a Gaussian start sheds some fraction of its energy before settling (sec. III A); previously identified quasi-breathers are periodic solutions with incoming radiation | [Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934), introduction, sec. III A, conclusion | READ |
| The I-ball/oscillon minimises its time-averaged energy for a given adiabatic charge I; I is approximately conserved when the quadratic part of the potential dominates (non-relativistic limit); the decay comes from the deviation of the potential from the quadratic one, where I is not precisely conserved | [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130), secs. II B, V (re-read) | READ |
| 3+1D symmetric double-well phi^4, Gaussian bubble start of radius r0: 125 resonances in the lifetime for 2.0 to 5.0; near each, the lifetime is of the form gamma ln abs(r0 - r0*) on either side with gamma_plus about gamma_minus and gamma varying between resonances; the solutions on resonance appear to have one unstable shape mode (a one-mode unstable intermediate attractor), by analogy with Type I critical collapse of a massive scalar (Brady et al., not opened), and gamma is expected to be the reciprocal Lyapunov exponent of that mode; the large-period amplitude modulation of typical oscillons disappears late in the life of those near a resonance; field period 4.6 in their units | [Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065), secs. on resonances and time scaling | READ |
| The same logarithmic scaling is reported for a double-well scalar coupled to gravity, with a modulation; the flat-space scaling is attributed to Honda & Choptuik and to Fodor et al. (neither re-read for this point beyond Honda & Choptuik) | [Ikeda, Yoo, Cardoso 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.01344), introduction, secs. on lifetime | READ |
| Complex-field Q-balls, piecewise-parabolic (omega_c about 0.960, m = 1) and thin-wall sextic potentials: the decay mode of the unstable branch (omega above the cusp frequency) continues analytically below the cusp as a specific vibrational mode that exists for frequencies close enough to omega_c; the vibrational spectra of the cases treated share common properties; linear perturbation theory, no nonlinear run | [Kovtun, Nugaev, Shkerin 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.03518), abstract, sec. 3, conclusion | READ |
| A squashed or stretched Q-ball (1+1D complex phi^6, beta = 1/4, omega = sqrt(3)/2, lambda = 1.05) keeps its charge; the perturbation decays with a rate that slows, in a pattern like t^(-1/2) (the Manton-Merabet law of the phi^4 kink shape mode); relaxation is dominated by the decay of a normal mode through nonlinear coupling to scattering modes and a long-lasting quasi-normal mode | [Ciurla et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06591), abstract, sec. 3.5 (re-read) | READ |
| The amplitude of the wobbling mode of the phi^4 kink decays as t^(-1/2) through second-harmonic radiation; the decay is appreciable only after times of order the inverse square of the initial amplitude | [Barashenkov & Oxtoby 2009](https://arxiv.org/abs/0907.3611), abstract, decay-law section | READ |
| The radiation losses of the wobbling kink can be compensated by resonant driving (direct or parametric), with multistability and hysteresis of the amplitude | [Oxtoby & Barashenkov 2009](https://arxiv.org/abs/0907.3615), abstract and introduction | READ |
| In 1+1D phi^4 with an external potential an oscillon and the oscillational mode of the kink are hard to distinguish over moderately long evolution, and the mode decays by the Manton-Merabet power law | [Romanczukiewicz & Shnir 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.09234), abstract, secs. 2.3, 3, A.1 | READ |
| 1+1D quasi-quadratic potential (m = 5), Gaussian start A_0 exp(-x^2/10): strongly excited oscillons are multi-oscillon quasi-bound states; their decay is chaotic and fractal in A_0 like kink-antikink collisions in phi^4, through resonant energy transfer from the amplitude modulation of the constituents, which are themselves read as two quasi-breathers in motion | [Blaschke et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00443), motivation, secs. II, III | READ |

### H3. Corrections to the Session 3 brief and the earlier addenda

- **N31 (the brief's ceiling).** N31 said "the relaxation sheds more N, so the true threshold is lower" than 1 - N_min/N. The scan shows this is wrong for field kicks and core removal at the two 4/3 starts when N is read at the kick (the thresholds lie 0.009 to 0.015 beyond the ceiling with the corrected primary N/N_min of 1.754): relaxation returns 1.6 to 2.1% of N there and sheds 3.4 to 3.6% after velocity kicks. In energy the argument holds: with E/(mN) of about 1 at N_min the ceilings in dE/E are -0.439, -0.417 and -0.489 and every threshold is inside them (`FAILURES.md` F-023). The wrong step was the choice of N at the kick as the coordinate and the unchecked sign of the relaxation correction, which the build's own linearity run (relaxed slope 1.908 epsilon against 1.986 epsilon at t = 0, field kicks) already contradicted.
- **N32 (the dip and the sign).** Applied to the dip start only. The primary start sits at N/N_min 1.754, 4.1% below the dip start (1.8295), on the low-N flank of the same dip, so adding N moves it into the dip (F-025).
- **N33 (the 3 omega source).** Correct for packets that leave the lump inside the dip (|dN/N| below about 0.5 to 1%, ratio primary over dip above 2); the written prediction had no amplitude limit (F-026).
- **N17 (omega at the N-minimum).** The line printing "0.9692 at 4/3" disagrees with the formula sqrt(1 - 0.0788/beta) = 0.9700 and with the next paragraph's 0.970; the b5 value 0.9921 agrees.
